<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<exited with exit code 0>
--- RESULT ---
<a id="windowing"></a>
## Windowing: Window.Current / ApplicationView / CoreWindow → AppWindow`Window.Current`, `ApplicationView`, and `CoreWindow` are gone. Track the main window yourself and use `Microsoft.UI.Windowing.AppWindow`. See the official [windowing guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/windowing).UWP (replace this):```csharp
var win = Window.Current;
ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView().TryResizeView(new Size(800, 600));
```WinUI 3 — expose `MainWindow` from `App`:```csharp
public partial class App : Application
{
    // With <Nullable>enable</Nullable>, declare as nullable since the assignment only happens during OnLaunched.
    public static Window? MainWindow { get; private set; }    protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
    {
        var window = new MainWindow();
        MainWindow = window;
        window.Activate();
    }
}
```Consumers running after `OnLaunched` can safely null-forgive:```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow!);
```Resize via `AppWindow`:```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
var windowId = Microsoft.UI.Win32Interop.GetWindowIdFromWindow(hwnd);
var appWindow = AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId);
appWindow.Resize(new SizeInt32(800, 600));
```### Initialization order — keep MainWindow's constructor inert`App.MainWindow` (or any equivalent static window reference) is `null` for the entire duration of `new MainWindow()` — the right-hand side runs to completion BEFORE the assignment happens. Anything inside that constructor that reads the static — directly or transitively — sees `null` and throws `E_POINTER` (0x80004003) at startup. The build is clean; the analyzer is silent; only a runtime launch reveals it.APIs that commonly trip this race when called from a `Page` reached during `MainWindow` construction:- `WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow)`
- `XamlRoot` / `Content.XamlRoot` access through a static-window helper
- `PrintManagerInterop.GetForWindow(hwnd)` / `ShowPrintUIForWindowAsync(hwnd)`
- `FileOpenPicker` / `FileSavePicker` / `FolderPicker` (require an HWND)
- `ContentDialog.ShowAsync()` when the dialog's `XamlRoot` is sourced from a static
- `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(...)` chained through `App.MainWindow`
- Any custom title bar / `SetTitleBar` helper that resolves the HWND staticallySafe pattern — assign before activate, defer first navigation:```csharp
// App.xaml.cs
protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
{
    var window = new MainWindow();
    MainWindow = window;
    window.Activate();
}
```

The `MainWindow` constructor must do only `InitializeComponent` and inert setup — no navigation to a `Page` that reads `
`App.MainWindow`. Defer the first navigation until after `Activate` completes, either from a `Loaded` handler hooked in `
`InitializeComponent` or by exposing a `NavigateToInitialPage()` method and calling it from `App.OnLaunched` after the st
tatic is assigned.

```csharp
// MainWindow.xaml.cs
public MainWindow()
{
    InitializeComponent();
    this.Activated += (_, _) =>
    {
        if (!_navigated)
        {
            _navigated = true;
            RootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage));
        }
    };
}
private bool _navigated;
```

Validator catches this race with a 10s smoke launch after the build healthcheck passes — see `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1`
` Section 7.

### AppWindow API replacements

| UWP API | WinUI 3 API |
|---------|-------------|
| `ApplicationView.TryResizeView` | `AppWindow.Resize` |
| `AppWindow.TryCreateAsync` | `AppWindow.Create` |
| `AppWindow.TryShowAsync` | `AppWindow.Show` |
| `AppWindow.TryConsolidateAsync` | `AppWindow.Destroy` |
| `AppWindow.RequestMoveXxx` | `AppWindow.Move` |
| `AppWindow.RequestPresentation` | `AppWindow.SetPresenter` |
| `CoreApplicationViewTitleBar` | `AppWindowTitleBar` |
| `CoreApplicationView.TitleBar.ExtendViewIntoTitleBar` | `AppWindow.TitleBar.ExtendsContentIntoTitleBar` |
<exited with exit code 0>
--- RESULT ---
<a id="getforcurrentview"></a>
## GetForCurrentView() ReplacementsNone of the `GetForCurrentView()` patterns work in WinUI 3 desktop — there is no implicit per-view singleton.| UWP API | WinUI 3 Replacement |
|---------|---------------------|
| `ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId)` |
| `UIViewSettings.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow` properties (size, presenter) |
| `DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView()` | `XamlRoot.RasterizationScale` or Win32 `GetDpiForWindow` |
| `CoreApplication.GetCurrentView()` | Track windows manually in `App` |
| `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView()` | Wire back handling in `NavigationView` / `BackRequested` directly |
<exited with exit code 0>

23.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
24.         // as NotifyUser()
25.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
26. 
27.         private Accelerometer _accelerometer;
28.         private uint _desiredReportInterval;
29.         private DispatcherTimer _dispatcherTimer;
30. 
31.         public Scenario3_Polling()
32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34.         }
35. 
36.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
37.         {
38.             _accelerometer = Accelerometer.GetDefault(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType);
39.             if (_accelerometer != null)
40.             {
41.                 // Select a report interval that is both suitable for the purposes of the app and supported by the sensor.
42.                 // This value will be used later to activate the sensor.
43.                 _desiredReportInterval = Math.Max(_accelerometer.MinimumReportInterval, 16);
44. 
45.                 // Set up a DispatchTimer
46.                 _dispatcherTimer = new DispatcherTimer();
47.                 _dispatcherTimer.Tick += DisplayCurrentReading;
48.                 _dispatcherTimer.Interval = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(_desiredReportInterval);
49. 
50.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType + " accelerometer ready", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
51.                 ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = true;
52.             }
53.             else
54.             {
55.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType + " accelerometer not found", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
56.             }
57.         }
58. 
59.         protected override void OnNavigatingFrom(NavigatingCancelEventArgs e)
60.         {
61.             if (ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled)
62.             {
63.                 ScenarioDisable();
64.             }
65.         }
66. 
67.         /// <summary>
68.         /// This is the event handler for VisibilityChanged events. You would register for these notifications
69.         /// if handling sensor data when the app is not visible could cause unintended actions in the app.
70.         /// </summary>
71.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
72.         /// <param name="e">
73.         /// Event data that can be examined for the current visibility state.
74.         /// </param>
75.         private void VisibilityChanged(object sender, VisibilityChangedEventArgs e)
76.         {
77.             if (ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled)
78.             {
79.                 if (e.Visible)
80.                 {
81.                     // Re-enable sensor input (no need to restore the desired reportInterval... it is restored for us upon app resume)
82.                     _dispatcherTimer.Start();
83.                 }
84.                 else
85.                 {
86.                     // Disable sensor input (no need to restore the default reportInterval... resources will be released upon app suspension)
87.                     _dispatcherTimer.Stop();
88.                 }
89.             }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// This is the dispatcher callback.
94.         /// </summary>
95.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
96.         /// <param name="args"></param>
97.         private void DisplayCurrentReading(object sender, object args)
98.         {
99.             AccelerometerReading reading = _accelerometer.GetCurrentReading();
100.             if (reading != null)
101.             {
102.                 MainPage.SetReadingText(ScenarioOutput, reading);
103.             }
104.         }
105. 
106.         /// <summary>
107.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Enable' button.
108.         /// </summary>
109.         private void ScenarioEnable()
110.         {
111.             // Set the report interval to enable the sensor for polling
112.             _accelerometer.ReportInterval = _desiredReportInterval;
113. 
114.             // TODO[migrate-007]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
115.             Window.Current.VisibilityChanged += VisibilityChanged;
116.             _dispatcherTimer.Start();
117. 
118.             ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = false;
119.             ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled = true;
120.         }

123.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Disable' button.
124.         /// </summary>
125.         private void ScenarioDisable()
126.         {
127.             // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
128.             Window.Current.VisibilityChanged -= VisibilityChanged;
129. 
130.             // Stop the dispatcher
131.             _dispatcherTimer.Stop();
132. 
133.             // Restore the default report interval to release resources while the sensor is not in use
134.             _accelerometer.ReportInterval = 0;
135. 
136.             ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = true;
137.             ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled = false;

43.                 // This value will be used later to activate the sensor.
44.                 desiredReportInterval = Math.Max(accelerometer.MinimumReportInterval, 16);
45. 
46.                 // MaxBatchSize will be 0 if the accelerometer does not support batching.
47.                 uint maxLatency = accelerometer.MaxBatchSize * desiredReportInterval;
48.                 desiredReportLatency = Math.Min(maxLatency, 10000);
49.

57.         }

62.             {
63.                 ScenarioDisable();
64.             }
65.         }

26.     {
27.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
28.         // as NotifyUser()

30. 
31.         Accelerometer accelerometerOriginal;
32.         Accelerometer accelerometerReadingTransform;
33.         DisplayInformation displayInformation;
34. 
35.         public Scenario4_OrientationChanged()
36.         {
37.             this.InitializeComponent();
38.         }
39. 
40.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
41.         {
42.             // Get two instances of the accelerometer:
43.             // One that returns the raw accelerometer data
44.             accelerometerOriginal = Accelerometer.GetDefault(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType);
45.             // Other on which the 'ReadingTransform' is updated so that data returned aligns with the request transformation.
46.             accelerometerReadingTransform = Accelerometer.GetDefault(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType);
47. 
48.             if (accelerometerOriginal == null || accelerometerReadingTransform == null)
49.             {
50.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType + " accelerometer not found", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
51.             }
52.             else
53.             {
54.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType + " accelerometer ready", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
55.                 ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = true;
56.             }
57. 
58.             // Register for orientation change
59.             // TODO[migrate-009]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
60.             displayInformation = DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView();
61.             displayInformation.OrientationChanged += displayInformation_OrientationChanged;
62.         }
63. 
64.         protected override void OnNavigatingFrom(NavigatingCancelEventArgs e)
65.         {
66.             if (ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled)
67.             {
68.                 ScenarioDisable();
69.             }
70. 
71.             displayInformation.OrientationChanged -= displayInformation_OrientationChanged;
72.         }
73. 
74. 
75.         /// <summary>
76.         /// Invoked when there is a change in the display orientation.
77.         /// </summary>
78.         /// <param name="sender">
79.         /// DisplayInformation object from which the new Orientation can be determined
80.         /// </param>
81.         /// <param name="e"></param>
82.         void displayInformation_OrientationChanged(DisplayInformation sender, object args)
83.         {
84.             if (null != accelerometerReadingTransform)
85.             {
86.                 accelerometerReadingTransform.ReadingTransform = sender.CurrentOrientation;
87.             }
88.         }
89. 
90.         /// <summary>
91.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Enable' button.
92.         /// </summary>
93.         void ScenarioEnable()
94.         {
95.             // Establish the report interval
96.             accelerometerOriginal.ReportInterval = accelerometerOriginal.MinimumReportInterval;
97.             accelerometerReadingTransform.ReportInterval = accelerometerReadingTransform.MinimumReportInterval;
98. 
99.             // Establish the ReadingTransform to align with the current display orientation, so 
100.             // that the accelerometer data from 'accelerometerReadingTransform' will align with the 
101.             // current display orientation
102.             accelerometerReadingTransform.ReadingTransform = displayInformation.CurrentOrientation;
103. 
104.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
105.             Window.Current.VisibilityChanged += Current_VisibilityChanged;
106.             accelerometerOriginal.ReadingChanged += _accelerometerOriginal_ReadingChanged;
107.             accelerometerReadingTransform.ReadingChanged += _accelerometerReadingTransform_ReadingChanged;
108. 
109.             ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = false;
110.             ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled = true;
111.         }
112. 
113.         /// <summary>
114.         /// This is the event handler for ReadingChanged event of the 'accelerometerOriginal' and should 
115.         /// notify of the accelerometer reading changes.
116.         /// </summary>
117.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
118.         /// <param name="args">
119.         /// Event data represents the accelerometer reading in its original reference frame.
120.         /// </param>
121.         async void _accelerometerOriginal_ReadingChanged(Accelerometer sender, AccelerometerReadingChangedEventArgs args)
122.         {
123.             // TODO[migrate-011]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
124.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
125.             {
126.                 MainPage.SetReadingText(ScenarioOutputOriginal, args.Reading);
127.             });
128.         }
129.         
130. 
131.         /// <summary>
132.         /// This is the event handler for ReadingChanged event of the 'accelerometerReadingTransform' and should 
133.         /// notify of the accelerometer reading changes.
134.         /// </summary>
135.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
136.         /// <param name="args">
137.         /// Event data represents the accelerometer reading in its original reference frame.
138.         /// </param>
139.         async void _accelerometerReadingTransform_ReadingChanged(Accelerometer sender, AccelerometerReadingChangedEventArgs args)
140.         {
141.             // TODO[migrate-012]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
142.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
143.             {
144.                 MainPage.SetReadingText(ScenarioOutputReadingTransform, args.Reading);
145.             });
146.         }
147. 
148.         /// <summary>
149.         /// This is the event handler for VisibilityChanged events. You would register for these notifications
150.         /// if handling sensor data when the app is not visible could cause unintended actions in the app.
151.         /// </summary>
152.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
153.         /// <param name="e">
154.         /// Event data that can be examined for the current visibility state.
155.         /// </param>
156.         void Current_VisibilityChanged(object sender, VisibilityChangedEventArgs e)
157.         {
158.             if (ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled)
159.             {
160.                 if (e.Visible)
161.                 {
162.                     // Re-enable sensor input (no need to restore the desired reportInterval... it is restored for us upon app resume)
163.                     accelerometerOriginal.ReadingChanged += _accelerometerOriginal_ReadingChanged;
164.                     accelerometerReadingTransform.ReadingChanged += _accelerometerReadingTransform_ReadingChanged;
165.                 }
166.                 else
167.                 {
168.                     // Disable sensor input (no need to restore the default reportInterval... resources will be released upon app suspension)
169.                     accelerometerOriginal.ReadingChanged -= _accelerometerOriginal_ReadingChanged;
170.                     accelerometerReadingTransform.ReadingChanged -= _accelerometerReadingTransform_ReadingChanged;
171.                 }
172.             }
173.         }
174. 
175.         /// <summary>
176.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Disable' button.
177.         /// </summary>
178.         void ScenarioDisable()
179.         {
180.             // TODO[migrate-013]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
181.             Window.Current.VisibilityChanged -= Current_VisibilityChanged;
182.             accelerometerOriginal.ReadingChanged -= _accelerometerOriginal_ReadingChanged;
183.             accelerometerReadingTransform.ReadingChanged -= _accelerometerReadingTransform_ReadingChanged;
184. 
185.             // Restore the default report interval to release resources while the sensor is not in use
186.             accelerometerOriginal.ReportInterval = 0;
187.             accelerometerReadingTransform.ReportInterval = 0;
188. 
189.             ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = true;
190.             ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled = false;
191.         }
192.     }
193. }

48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)

51.             }

68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }
157.     public enum NotifyType
158.     {
159.         StatusMessage,
160.         ErrorMessage
161.     };
162. }
163.

Sensor device fallback (required by skill rules)\nEach scenario page that initializes the accelerometer already shows an error message via `rootPage.NotifyUser(..., NotifyType.ErrorMessage)` when the sensor is not found. This is the existing error handling. It shows \"accelerometer not found\" message. That satisfies the fallback requirement.\n\n### 8. VisibilityChangedEventArgs type\nIn WinUI 3, `Window.VisibilityChanged` uses `WindowVisibilityChangedEventArgs` (from `Microsoft.UI.Xaml`), not `VisibilityChangedEventArgs` (from `Windows.UI.Core`). Update all the `VisibilityChanged` handler signatures in scenarios 1-5. However, you can also use `object` for the event args type and cast if needed. The safest is:\n```csharp\n// Old UWP:\nprivate void VisibilityChanged(object sender, VisibilityChangedEventArgs e)\n// New WinUI 3:\nprivate void VisibilityChanged(object sender, WindowVisibilityChangedEventArgs e)\n```\n`e.Visible` exists on both types.\n\n### 9. Remove the `async` keyword and `await` from event handlers that only need DispatcherQueue\nWhen converting `await Dispatcher.RunAsync(...)` to `DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(...)`, the method no longer needs to be async. Remove `async` keyword and `await`.\n\n### Build and test approach\nAfter making changes, build using:\n```powershell\ncd C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run1\\000_Accelerometer\\app\\Accelerometer\n$arch = $env:PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE\n$Platform = if ($arch -eq 'AMD64') { 'x64' } else { $arch }\ndotnet build -c Debug -p:Platform=$Platform; \"BUILD_EXIT=$LASTEXITCODE\"\n```\nFix any build errors. Then run the validator:\n```powershell\n$skillRoot = \"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run1\\000_Accelerometer\\app\\.github\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\"\n& \"$skillRoot\\scripts\\Validate-UwpMigration.ps1\" -Target \"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run1\\000_Accelerometer\\app\\Accelerometer\"\n```\n\n### Important notes\n1. Do NOT run git operations\n2. Keep all scenario pages and their functionality - no silent omissions\n3. The `MainPage` in `SDKTemplate` namespace is the same object that `MainWindow` navigates to\n4. The WinUI 3 MainWindow already has `RootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage))` — update this to `typeof(SDKTemplate.MainPage)` using a fully qualified name or `using SDKTemplate;` \n5. The `SampleConfiguration.cs` already has the correct `SDKTemplate` namespace - don't modify it\n6. After all fixes, run the validator script and fix any issues it reports\n7. The deferred files (bin/obj artifacts) should not cause issues as they're in bin/obj dirs which SDK-style projects exclude\n8. `Windows.Graphics.Display.DisplayOrientations` is fine to keep using - it's a Windows platform API available to WinUI 3 apps\n\n### CRITICAL: Existing file conflict\nThe WinUI 3 project already has `MainPage.xaml` and `MainPage.xaml.cs` in the `Accelerometer` namespace. 45.             // Other on which the 'ReadingTransform' is updated so that data returned aligns with the request transformation.

86.         async private void ReadingChanged(object sender, AccelerometerReadingChangedEventArgs e)
87.         {
88.             // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
89.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
90.             {
91.                 MainPage.SetReadingText(ScenarioOutput, e.Reading);
92.             });
93.         }
94. 
95.         /// <summary>
96.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Enable' button.
97.         /// </summary>
98.         private void ScenarioEnable()
99.         {
100.             // Select a report interval that is both suitable for the purposes of the app and supported by the sensor.
101.             _accelerometer.ReportInterval = Math.Max(_accelerometer.MinimumReportInterval, 16);
102. 
103.             // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
104.             Window.Current.VisibilityChanged += VisibilityChanged;
105.             _accelerometer.ReadingChanged += ReadingChanged;
106. 
107.             ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = false;
108.             ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled = true;
109.         }
110. 
111.         /// <summary>
112.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Disable' button.
113.         /// </summary>
114.         private void ScenarioDisable()
115.         {
116.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
117.             Window.Current.VisibilityChanged -= VisibilityChanged;
118.             _accelerometer.ReadingChanged -= ReadingChanged;
119. 
120.             // Restore the default report interval to release resources while the sensor is not in use
121.             _accelerometer.ReportInterval = 0;
122. 
123.             ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = true;
124.             ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled = false;

29.         private uint desiredReportLatency;
30. 
31. 
32.         public Scenario5_DataEventsBatching()
33.         {
34.             this.InitializeComponent();
35.         }
36. 
37.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
38.         {
39.             accelerometer = Accelerometer.GetDefault(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType);
40.             if (accelerometer != null)
41.             {
42.                 // Select a report interval that is both suitable for the purposes of the app and supported by the sensor.
43.                 // This value will be used later to activate the sensor.
44.                 desiredReportInterval = Math.Max(accelerometer.MinimumReportInterval, 16);
45. 
46.                 // MaxBatchSize will be 0 if the accelerometer does not support batching.
47.                 uint maxLatency = accelerometer.MaxBatchSize * desiredReportInterval;
48.                 desiredReportLatency = Math.Min(maxLatency, 10000);
49. 
50.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType + " accelerometer ready", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
51.                 ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = true;
52.             }
53.             else
54.             {
55.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType + " accelerometer not found", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
56.             }
57.         }
58. 
59.         protected override void OnNavigatingFrom(NavigatingCancelEventArgs e)
60.         {
61.             if (ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled)
62.             {
63.                 ScenarioDisable();
64.             }
65.         }
66. 
67.         /// <summary>
68.         /// This is the event handler for VisibilityChanged events. You would register for these notifications
69.         /// if handling sensor data when the app is not visible could cause unintended actions in the app.
70.         /// </summary>
71.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
72.         /// <param name="e">
73.         /// Event data that can be examined for the current visibility state.
74.         /// </param>
75.         private void VisibilityChanged(object sender, VisibilityChangedEventArgs e)
76.         {
77.             if (ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled)
78.             {
79.                 if (e.Visible)
80.                 {
81.                     // Re-enable sensor input (no need to restore the desired reportInterval... it is restored for us upon app resume)
82.                     accelerometer.ReadingChanged += ReadingChanged;
83.                 }
84.                 else
85.                 {
86.                     // Disable sensor input (no need to restore the default reportInterval... resources will be released upon app suspension)
87.                     accelerometer.ReadingChanged -= ReadingChanged;
88.                 }
89.             }
90.         }

21.     public sealed partial class Scenario3_Polling : Page
22.     {
23.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
24.         // as NotifyUser()
25.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
26. 
27.         private Accelerometer _accelerometer;
28.         private uint _desiredReportInterval;
29.         private DispatcherTimer _dispatcherTimer;
30. 
31.         public Scenario3_Polling()
32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34.         }
35. 
36.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
37.         {
38.             _accelerometer = Accelerometer.GetDefault(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType);
39.             if (_accelerometer != null)
40.             {
41.                 // Select a report interval that is both suitable for the purposes of the app and supported by the sensor.
42.                 // This value will be used later to activate the sensor.
43.                 _desiredReportInterval = Math.Max(_accelerometer.MinimumReportInterval, 16);
44. 
45.                 // Set up a DispatchTimer
46.                 _dispatcherTimer = new DispatcherTimer();
47.                 _dispatcherTimer.Tick += DisplayCurrentReading;
48.                 _dispatcherTimer.Interval = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(_desiredReportInterval);
49. 
50.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType + " accelerometer ready", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
51.                 ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = true;
52.             }
53.             else
54.             {
55.                 rootPage.NotifyUser(rootPage.AccelerometerReadingType + " accelerometer not found", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
56.             }
57.         }
58. 
59.         protected override void OnNavigatingFrom(NavigatingCancelEventArgs e)
60.         {
61.             if (ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled)
62.             {
63.                 ScenarioDisable();
64.             }
65.         }
66. 
67.         /// <summary>
68.         /// This is the event handler for VisibilityChanged events. You would register for these notifications
69.         /// if handling sensor data when the app is not visible could cause unintended actions in the app.
70.         /// </summary>
71.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
72.         /// <param name="e">
73.         /// Event data that can be examined for the current visibility state.
74.         /// </param>
75.         private void VisibilityChanged(object sender, VisibilityChangedEventArgs e)
76.         {
77.             if (ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled)
78.             {
79.                 if (e.Visible)
80.                 {
81.                     // Re-enable sensor input (no need to restore the desired reportInterval... it is restored for us upon app resume)
82.                     _dispatcherTimer.Start();
83.                 }
84.                 else
85.                 {
86.                     // Disable sensor input (no need to restore the default reportInterval... resources will be released upon app suspension)
87.                     _dispatcherTimer.Stop();
88.                 }
89.             }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// This is the dispatcher callback.
94.         /// </summary>
95.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
96.         /// <param name="args"></param>
97.         private void DisplayCurrentReading(object sender, object args)
98.         {
99.             AccelerometerReading reading = _accelerometer.GetCurrentReading();
100.             if (reading != null)
101.             {
102.                 MainPage.SetReadingText(ScenarioOutput, reading);
103.             }
104.         }
105. 
106.         /// <summary>
107.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Enable' button.
108.         /// </summary>
109.         private void ScenarioEnable()
110.         {
111.             // Set the report interval to enable the sensor for polling
112.             _accelerometer.ReportInterval = _desiredReportInterval;
113. 
114.             // TODO[migrate-007]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
115.             Window.Current.VisibilityChanged += VisibilityChanged;
116.             _dispatcherTimer.Start();
117. 
118.             ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = false;
119.             ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled = true;
120.         }
121. 
122.         /// <summary>
123.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Disable' button.
124.         /// </summary>
125.         private void ScenarioDisable()
126.         {
127.             // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
128.             Window.Current.VisibilityChanged -= VisibilityChanged;
129. 
130.             // Stop the dispatcher
131.             _dispatcherTimer.Stop();
132. 
133.             // Restore the default report interval to release resources while the sensor is not in use
134.             _accelerometer.ReportInterval = 0;
135. 
136.             ScenarioEnableButton.IsEnabled = true;
137.             ScenarioDisableButton.IsEnabled = false;
138.         }

<a id="startup-crashes"></a>
## Diagnosing Startup CrashesA packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash the instant it launches — `winapp run` returns no usable window and the process is gone. The build log and the analyzer cannot see this; only a real launch does. The **wrong** way to chase it is to sprinkle `File.WriteAllText` traces through `Program.cs` / `App.xaml.cs` and re-run repeatedly — that burns time and usually misses the real frame (the crash often happens in native activation, before your managed `Main` even runs).**Capture the real exception first.** Either run the launch-and-diagnose helper:```powershell
& "<skill-root>/scripts/Test-AppLaunch.ps1" -Target "<winui3-project-root>"
```…or read the Windows **Application** event log directly for the two events the crash writes:```powershell
Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 40 |
  Where-Object { $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) } |
  ForEach-Object { "`n=== Event $($_.Id) @ $($_.TimeCreated) ==="; $_.Message }
```- **Event 1000** — faulting module + native **Exception code** (e.g. `0x8001010E`).
- **Event 1026** — *.NET Runtime*: the managed **exception type, message, and stack**. This is the one that names the actual throwing frame. Read it before changing any code.**Known native codes and what they usually mean** (the code names the error *class*; the real fix is whatever the captured stack points at):| Exception code | Meaning | Usual migration cause → where to look |
|---|---|---|
| `0x80004003` | `E_POINTER` | Static-window **init-order race** — a `Page` read `App.MainWindow` (or another static window reference) before `OnLaunched` assigned it. Keep `MainWindow`'s constructor inert and navigate after `Activate`. See [Initialization order](#windowing). |
| `0x8001010E` | `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` | A **thread/apartment-affined object** was accessed during startup — commonly a view- or `CoreWindow`-affined UWP API touched from a `static` initializer, a type constructor, or off the UI thread. Construct/access it on the UI thread *after* `Activate`. If the API has no WinUI 3 desktop equivalent, defer it. |
| `0xE0434352` | Managed CLR exception | Read the **.NET exception type** in event 1026. `TypeLoadException` / `FileNotFoundException` almost always means a missing or version-incompatible package reference, not your code. |
| `0xC000027B` | Native stowed exception | Often a legacy projection/activation incompatibility for an API used at startup. If the API/contract is unsupported on the current OS, defer it. |> Do **not** assume the entry point is the problem. A custom `Program.Main` for WinUI 3 **correctly** carries `[STAThread]` + `ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers()` + the `DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext` setup — this matches the SDK's auto-generated `Main`. `[STAThread]` is **required**, not a bug. If you have a hand-written entry point and don't need single-instancing/redirection, the simplest path is to delete it and let the SDK generate `Main`.The Step 4 validator runs this same check (`Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7) and **fails** when the app registers but dies at startup, surfacing the captured signature in `.validator-diagnostics.txt`.

Let me look at the Test-AppLaunch script to understand the launch mechanism:

--- TOOL: view ---
{"path":"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run1\\000_Accelerometer\\app\\.github\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\\scripts\\Test-AppLaunch.ps1"}
--- RESULT ---
1. <#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3. Launch an already-built WinUI 3 app and report whether it survives startup -
4. and if not, capture the REAL unhandled-exception signature from Windows Error
5. Reporting so a startup crash can be diagnosed immediately instead of guessed at.
6. 
7. .DESCRIPTION
8. A packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash on startup. When that
9. happens the only durable signal is the Windows Application event log: event 1000
10. (faulting module + native exception code) and event 1026 (.NET Runtime - the
11. managed exception TYPE, message, and stack). This script launches the built
12. layout via 'winapp run --detach --json', waits for the cold-start window, and:
13. 
14.   - if the process is alive  -> status=running  (exit 0)
15.   - if it registered then died/threw at startup -> status=crashed (exit 1),
16.     with the captured WER signature + a pointer to PATTERNS.md#startup-crashes
17.   - if it never registered (deploy/env failure: Developer Mode off, missing
18.     framework, MAX_PATH, no winapp) -> status=unavailable (exit 2) - inconclusive,
19.     NOT a code defect.
20. 
21. It NEVER throws on an operational failure; it returns a structured result and
22. (with -Json) prints it as JSON. This is the single source of truth for the
23. "did the migrated app launch" question - both Step 3 (interactive, when the app
24. first crashes) and the Step 4 validator (Section 7) call it.
25. 
26. WHY THIS MATTERS: the common failure mode is an agent that sees a startup crash
27. and hand-rolls File.WriteAllText tracing for the rest of its budget. Don't. Run
28. this once; read the captured managed exception; fix the named frame.
29. 
30. NOTE: this script is intentionally ASCII-only. The Step 4 validator invokes it
31. via 'powershell -File' (Windows PowerShell 5.1), which reads a UTF-8-no-BOM file
32. as ANSI; non-ASCII characters would corrupt parsing under 5.1.
33. 
34. .PARAMETER Target
35. The migrated WinUI 3 project root (the folder with the .csproj). The build-output
36. layout is discovered under bin/<arch>/Debug/<tfm>/win-<rid>. Use this OR -Layout.
37. 
38. .PARAMETER Layout
39. An explicit build-output layout folder (the one containing AppxManifest.xml +
40. the app .exe, or an AppX\ subfolder). Use this OR -Target.
41. 
42. .PARAMETER SettleSeconds
43. Seconds to wait after launch before the alive check. Default 10 - enough to
44. catch OnLaunched / first-Page-navigation crashes without bloating runtime.
45. 
46. .PARAMETER Json
47. Emit the structured result as JSON to stdout (for programmatic callers).
48. 
49. .OUTPUTS
50. PSCustomObject (and JSON with -Json):
51.   { ok, status, pid, aumid, layout, crash:{ code, module, managedType, message,
52.     hint, anchor } | $null, detail }
53. status in { running, crashed, unavailable }.  exit: 0 running / 1 crashed / 2 unavailable.
54. 
55. .EXAMPLE
56. .\Test-AppLaunch.ps1 -Target "C:\out\MyWinUI3App"
57. # Interactive: launch and, on crash, print the real exception + the fix pointer.
58. 
59. .EXAMPLE
60. $r = .\Test-AppLaunch.ps1 -Layout $launchFolder -Json | ConvertFrom-Json
61. if ($r.status -eq 'crashed') { "FAIL: $($r.crash.hint)" }
62. #>
63. [CmdletBinding(DefaultParameterSetName = 'Target')]
64. param(
65.     [Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName = 'Target')][string]$Target,
66.     [Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName = 'Layout')][string]$Layout,
67.     [int]$SettleSeconds = 10,
68.     [switch]$Json
69. )
70. 
71. # This script reports failures structurally; it must not abort on a non-zero
72. # external tool exit. Errors are handled explicitly per stage.
73. $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
74. 
75. function New-LaunchResult {
76.     param([bool]$Ok, [string]$Status, [string]$Detail, [hashtable]$Extra)
77.     $r = [ordered]@{
78.         ok = $Ok; status = $Status; pid = $null; aumid = $null
79.         layout = $null; crash = $null; detail = $Detail
80.     }
81.     if ($Extra) { foreach ($k in $Extra.Keys) { $r[$k] = $Extra[$k] } }
82.     [pscustomobject]$r
83. }
84. 
85. function Write-LaunchOut {
86.     param([pscustomobject]$Result)
87.     # exit code: running=0, crashed=1, unavailable=2
88.     $exit = switch ($Result.status) { 'running' { 0 } 'crashed' { 1 } default { 2 } }
89.     if ($Json) {
90.         $Result | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6
91.     } else {
92.         $tag = switch ($Result.status) { 'running' { 'OK  ' } 'crashed' { 'FAIL' } default { 'WARN' } }
93.         Write-Host ""
94.         Write-Host "==> Test-AppLaunch [$tag] status=$($Result.status)"
95.         if ($Result.layout) { Write-Host "    Layout : $($Result.layout)" }
96.         if ($Result.pid)    { Write-Host "    PID    : $($Result.pid)   AUMID: $($Result.aumid)" }
97.         if ($Result.crash) {
98.             Write-Host "    Crash  : code=$($Result.crash.code)  module=$($Result.crash.module)"
99.             if ($Result.crash.managedType) { Write-Host "    .NET   : $($Result.crash.managedType)" }
100.             if ($Result.crash.message)     { Write-Host "    Message: $($Result.crash.message)" }
101.             if ($Result.crash.hint)        { Write-Host "    Hint   : $($Result.crash.hint)" }
102.             if ($Result.crash.anchor)      { Write-Host "    Fix    : Get-MigrationPattern.ps1 -Anchor $($Result.crash.anchor)" }
103.         }
104.         if ($Result.detail) { Write-Host "    Detail : $($Result.detail)" }
105.     }
106.     exit $exit
107. }
108. 
109. # ---- Resolve the launch layout ----------------------------------------------
110. $exeNames = @()
111. if ($PSCmdlet.ParameterSetName -eq 'Target') {
112.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Target)) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Target not found: $Target") }
113.     $Target = (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Target).Path
114.     $csproj = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Target -Recurse -Filter *.csproj -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
115.         Where-Object { $_.FullName -notmatch '\\(bin|obj|\.uwp-source)\\' } | Select-Object -First 1 -ExpandProperty FullName
116.     if (-not $csproj) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "No .csproj found under $Target") }
117.     $csprojDir = Split-Path -Parent $csproj
118.     $arch = if ($env:PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE -eq 'ARM64') { 'ARM64' } else { 'x64' }
119.     $rid = $arch.ToLower()
120.     # Walk bin/<arch>/Debug and one level into the newest TFM dir, then win-<rid>.
121.     $binCandidates = @(
122.         (Join-Path $csprojDir "bin\$arch\Debug"),
123.         (Join-Path $csprojDir "bin\$rid\Debug")
124.     )
125.     foreach ($bin in $binCandidates) {
126.         if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $bin)) { continue }
127.         $tfmDir = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $bin -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
128.             Sort-Object LastWriteTime -Descending | Select-Object -First 1
129.         if (-not $tfmDir) { continue }
130.         $ridDir = Join-Path $tfmDir.FullName "win-$rid"
131.         $Layout = if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $ridDir) { $ridDir } else { $tfmDir.FullName }
132.         break
133.     }
134.     if (-not $Layout) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "No build output found under bin\$arch\Debug - build the project first.") }
135. }
136. 
137. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Layout)) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout folder not found: $Layout") }
138. $Layout = (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Layout).Path
139. 
140. # Sanity: the layout needs an exe + a manifest, else winapp run can't launch it.
141. $exes = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Layout -Filter '*.exe' -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
142. $exeNames = @($exes | ForEach-Object { [System.IO.Path]::GetFileNameWithoutExtension($_.Name) })
143. $hasMfst = (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppxManifest.xml')) -or
144.            (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppX\AppxManifest.xml'))
145. if ($exes.Count -eq 0 -or -not $hasMfst) {
146.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout is incomplete (need an .exe + AppxManifest.xml): $Layout" @{ layout = $Layout })
147. }
148. if (-not (Get-Command winapp -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
149.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "winapp CLI not on PATH - cannot launch the packaged app." @{ layout = $Layout })
150. }
151. 
152. # ---- Launch ------------------------------------------------------------------
153. $runRaw = & winapp run "$Layout" --detach --json 2>&1 | Out-String
154. $run = $null
155. try { $run = $runRaw.Trim() | ConvertFrom-Json -ErrorAction Stop } catch { }
156. $thePid = $null; $aumid = $null; $runErr = $null
157. if ($run) {
158.     foreach ($p in 'ProcessId','PID','Pid') { if ($run.$p) { $thePid = [int]$run.$p; break } }
159.     if ($run.AUMID) { $aumid = [string]$run.AUMID }
160.     if ($run.Error) { $runErr = [string]$run.Error }
161. }
162. 
163. # ---- WER capture helper ------------------------------------------------------
164. # Reads the real unhandled-exception signature for the just-crashed app:
165. #   event 1000  -> faulting module + native Exception code (e.g. 0x8001010E)
166. #   event 1026  -> .NET Runtime: managed exception TYPE + message + stack
167. # Match on the layout's exe base name(s) within a short recent window.
168. function Get-CrashSignature {
169.     param([string[]]$Names)
170.     $sig = [ordered]@{ code = $null; module = $null; managedType = $null; message = $null; hint = $null; anchor = $null }
171.     if (-not $Names -or $Names.Count -eq 0) { return $sig }
172.     $namePattern = ($Names | ForEach-Object { [regex]::Escape($_ + '.exe') }) -join '|'
173.     $events = Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 80 -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
174.         Where-Object { $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) -and $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.Message -match $namePattern }
175. 
176.     $e1000 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1000 } | Select-Object -First 1
177.     if ($e1000) {
178.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Faulting module name:\s*([^,]+)') { $sig.module = $matches[1].Trim() }
179.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Exception code:\s*(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)') { $sig.code = $matches[1].ToLower() }
180.     }
181.     $e1026 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1026 } | Select-Object -First 1
182.     if ($e1026) {
183.         # ".NET Runtime" event: "Exception Info: <Type>: <message>"
184.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception)') { $sig.managedType = $matches[1].Trim() }
185.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*[A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception[^\r\n:]*:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
186.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
187.         } elseif ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
188.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
189.         }
190.     }
191. 
192.     # Map the native exception code to a conservative hint + pattern anchor.
193.     # These name the error CLASS and point at the captured stack / the doc; they
194.     # do not assert a single fix, since the real frame is in the captured exception.
195.     switch ($sig.code) {
196.         '0x80004003' {
197.             $sig.hint   = "E_POINTER - most often the static-window init-order race: a Page read App.MainWindow (or another static window ref) before OnLaunched assigned it. Keep MainWindow's ctor inert; navigate after Activate."
198.             $sig.anchor = 'windowing'
199.         }
200.         '0x8001010e' {
201.             $sig.hint   = "RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD - a thread/apartment-affined object was accessed during startup (commonly a view/CoreWindow-affined UWP API touched from a static initializer or off the UI thread). Construct/access it on the UI thread after Activate; if the API is unsupported on WinUI 3 desktop, defer it."
202.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
203.         }
204.         '0xe0434352' {
205.             $sig.hint   = "Managed .NET exception - see the .NET exception type captured above (event 1026). Resolve that type (e.g. TypeLoad / FileNotFound usually means a missing or incompatible package reference)."
206.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
207.         }
208.         '0xc000027b' {
209.             $sig.hint   = "Native stowed exception - frequently a legacy projection/activation incompatibility. If a UWP API/contract used at startup is unsupported on this OS, defer it per MIGRATION-DEFERRED.md."
210.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
211.         }
212.         default {
213.             $sig.hint   = "Startup crash - read the captured exception (event 1026) to find the throwing frame."
214.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
215.         }
216.     }
217.     return $sig
218. }
219. 
220. # ---- Classify ----------------------------------------------------------------
221. if ($thePid) {
222.     Start-Sleep -Seconds $SettleSeconds
223.     $alive = $null -ne (Get-Process -Id $thePid -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
224.     if ($alive) {
225.         # Best-effort cleanup so we don't leave the dev-registered app running.
226.         try { Stop-Process -Id $thePid -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue } catch {}
227.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $true 'running' "App stayed alive ${SettleSeconds}s after launch (pid $thePid)." @{ layout = $Layout; pid = $thePid; aumid = $aumid })
228.     } else {
229.         $crash = Get-CrashSignature -Names $exeNames
230.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'crashed' "App launched (pid $thePid) but exited within ${SettleSeconds}s - startup crash." @{ layout = $Layout; aumid = $aumid; crash = [pscustomobject]$crash })
231.     }
232. } else {
233.     # No PID. Distinguish a startup crash from a deploy/env failure by whether the
234.     # app actually got REGISTERED/ACTIVATED (winapp emits an AUMID once it does).
235.     if ($aumid) {
236.         # Registered + activated, then threw before it could report a PID => startup crash (DEFECT).
237.         $crash = Get-CrashSignature -Names $exeNames
238.         $detail = "App registered (AUMID present) but crashed during activation/startup"
239.         if ($runErr) { $detail = $detail + " - winapp reported '" + $runErr + "'" }
240.         $detail = $detail + "."
241.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'crashed' $detail @{ layout = $Layout; aumid = $aumid; crash = [pscustomobject]$crash })
242.     } else {
243.         # Never registered => deployment/environment problem, not a code defect. Inconclusive.
244.         $detail = "winapp run did not register the app (no AUMID, no PID)"
245.         if ($runErr) { $detail = $detail + " - '" + $runErr + "'" }
246.         $detail = $detail + ". Usually Developer Mode off, a missing framework dependency, cert issue, or MAX_PATH - not a migration defect."
247.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' $detail @{ layout = $Layout })
248.     }
249. }
250.

<Project>
  <PropertyGroup>
    <AddProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocalDependsOn>
      ContentFilesProjectOutputGroup;
      Prep_ComputeProcessXamlFiles;
      $(AddProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocalDependsOn)
    </AddProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocalDependsOn>
  </PropertyGroup>  <Target Name="AddProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocal" Condition="'$(AppxPackage)' != 'true'" DependsOnTargets="$(AddProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocalDependsOn)" BeforeTargets="GetCopyToOutputDirectoryItems">
    <ItemGroup>
      <_ProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocal Include="@(GeneratedXamlSrc->'%(FullPath)')" />
      <_ProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocal Condition="'$(GenerateLibraryLayout)' == 'true' and '$(DisableXbfGeneration)' != 'true'" Include="@(GeneratedXamlSrc0->'%(FullPath)')" />
    </ItemGroup>    <AssignTargetPath Files="@(_ProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocal)" RootFolder="$(XamlGeneratedOutputPath)">
      <Output TaskParameter="AssignedFiles" ItemName="_ProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocalWithTargetPath" />
    </AssignTargetPath>    <ItemGroup>
      <_AllChildProjectItemsWithTargetPath Include="@(_ProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocalWithTargetPath)" Condition="'%(Extension)' != '.xbf' OR ('%(Extension)' == '.xbf' AND '$(_SupportXbfAsEmbedFileResources)' != 'true')">
        <TargetPath Condition="'$(PriInitialPath)' != ''">$(PriInitialPath)\%(_ProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocalWithTargetPath.TargetPath)</TargetPath>
        <CopyToOutputDirectory>PreserveNewest</CopyToOutputDirectory>
      </_AllChildProjectItemsWithTargetPath>
    </ItemGroup>    <ItemGroup>
      <_AllChildProjectItemsWithTargetPath Include="@(ContentFilesProjectOutputGroupOutput)">
        <TargetPath Condition="'$(PriInitialPath)' != ''">$(PriInitialPath)\%(ContentFilesProjectOutputGroupOutput.TargetPath)</TargetPath>
        <CopyToOutputDirectory>PreserveNewest</CopyToOutputDirectory>
      </_AllChildProjectItemsWithTargetPath>
    </ItemGroup>
    
    <Message Importance="Low" Text="(Out) _ProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocal == @(_ProcessedXamlFilesToCopyLocal)" />
    <Message Importance="Low" Text="(Out) _XamlFilesToCopyLocal == @(_XamlFilesToCopyLocal)" />
  </Target>  <!-- Allow nuget pack task to pick up .xaml files -->
  <PropertyGroup>
    <IncludeXamlFilesInNugetPackage Condition="'$(OutputType)'=='Library' and '$(IncludeXamlFilesInNugetPackage)'==''">true</IncludeXamlFilesInNugetPackage>
    <AllowedOutputExtensionsInPackageBuildOutputFolder Condition="'$(IncludeXamlFilesInNugetPackage)'=='true'">$(AllowedOutputExtensionsInPackageBuildOutputFolder);.xaml</AllowedOutputExtensionsInPackageBuildOutputFolder>
  </PropertyGroup>    <!--
      Add xaml files to BuiltProjectOutputGroupOutput, so that it will get included in the NuGet package by the Pack target.
      Even though this is called the build output group, we use the Page items to avoid an extra copy
    -->
  <Target Name="AddXamlFilesToNugetPackage"
          BeforeTargets="BuiltProjectOutputGroup"
          Condition="'$(IncludeXamlFilesInNugetPackage)'=='true'">
    <ItemGroup>
    <!--
      If a library package doesn't want to pack a specific xaml file for some reason, they can update
      the metadata on the item so that it isn't packed:

      <ItemGroup>
         <Page Update="MyUserControl.xaml" Pack="false"/>
      </ItemGroup>
    -->
      <BuiltProjectOutputGroupOutput Include="@(Page)" Condition="'%(Page.Pack)'!='False'" KeepMetadata="False"
                                     TargetPath="$(AssemblyName)\%(Identity)"
                                     FinalOutputPath="$(MSBuildProjectDirectory)\%(Identity)"/>
    </ItemGroup>
  </Target>

</Project>

    By default, the Nuget targets for .NET projects copy MUI files next to the .dll.

  -->
  <Target Name="_UpdateMuiFilesAfterNugetRestore" AfterTargets="ResolveNuGetPackageAssets;ResolvePackageAssets">        
    <ItemGroup>
      <!-- .NET5 projects and this takes affect after the ResolvePackageAssets target -->

%(Directory)))))</DestinationSubDirectory>

            $([MSBuild]::MakeRelative($(_WinUIDllDir), %(RootDir)%(Directory)))))%(FileName)%(Extension)</DestinationSub
bPath>
      </_WinUINativeCopyLocalItems>

      <!--
        Placing these items in NativeCopyLocalItems results in a deps.json that the .NET runtime fails to load with erro
or:
          An assembly specified in the application dependencies manifest (WinUIGallery.deps.json) was not found:        
            package: 'Microsoft.WinUI', version: '3.0.0-xdev.1'
            path: 'runtimes/win10-x86/native/af-ZA/Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Phone.dll.mui'

        Moving these to RuntimeTargetsCopyLocalItems with the same metadata results in proper placement of files and a d
deps.json
        that can be loaded.
      -->
      <NativeCopyLocalItems Remove="@(_WinUINativeCopyLocalItems)"/>
      <RuntimeTargetsCopyLocalItems Include="@(_WinUINativeCopyLocalItems)"/>
      <_WinUINativeCopyLocalItems Remove="@(_WinUINativeCopyLocalItems)"/>

      <!--
        This for .NET Native projects only and takes affect after the ResolveNuGetPackageAssets.
        This can be removed once we no longer support .NET Native.
       -->
      <ReferenceCopyLocalPaths Update="@(ReferenceCopyLocalPaths)" Condition="'%(ReferenceCopyLocalPaths.NuGetPackageId)
)' == 'Microsoft.WinUI' and '%(Extension)' == '.mui' and
                                                                              '%(ReferenceCopyLocalPaths.DestinationSubD
Directory)'==''">
        <DestinationSubDirectory>$([MSBuild]::EnsureTrailingSlash($([MSBuild]::MakeRelative($(_WinUIDllDir), %(RootDir)%
%(Directory)))))</DestinationSubDirectory>
      </ReferenceCopyLocalPaths>
    </ItemGroup>
  </Target>

  <!-- By default, add CRT package dependencies for UAP apps.  Previously, this was done to satisfy
      CRT linkage for WinUI 3 binaries.  With Hybrid CRT, that's no longer needed, but we retain this behavior
      to avoid breaking existing projects.  At deployment time, it's very likely that a target device will already      
      have Microsoft.VCLibs and Microsoft.VCLibs.Desktop FWPs installed.  But apps can still opt out of referencing     
      these FWPs by setting WinUISDKReferences='false'. -->

        Because we set the TargetPlatformIdentifer to 'UAP' in our Desktop .vcxproj files, the .wapproj thinks these

        there's a bug where the TargetPath metadata doesn't have the language-specific subfolder. Our desktop projects  
        should have TargetPlatformIdentifier be set to Windows, and this would all just work, but there are other       
        issues preventing them from building that way.
        -->
      <WapProjPackageFile Include="@(_FilteredNonWapProjProjectOutput)" Condition="'%(Extension)' == '.mui'">

CopyToTargetPath>

The following policies define the servicing you can expect when you use a given Windows App SDK release.

### Release lifecycle

The Windows App SDK has a lifecycle. A lifecycle begins when a version or service is released and ends when it's no longer supported. Knowing key dates in this release lifecycle helps you make informed decisions about when to upgrade or make other changes to your software.

### Servicing

Customers can choose _Current_ releases or _Maintenance_ releases. Both types of releases receive critical fixes throughout their lifecycle, for security, compatibility, and reliability. You must stay up to date with the latest patches to qualify for support.

The quality of all releases is the same. The only difference is the servicing time frame.

#### Servicing for Current releases

Current releases are the latest stable release and they receive fixes more frequently. During the servicing period, Windows App SDK current release is updated to improve functional capabilities and mitigate security vulnerabilities.

## Windows App SDK release servicing policy

The following policies define the servicing you can expect when you use a given Windows App SDK release.

### Release lifecycle

The Windows App SDK has a lifecycle. A lifecycle begins when a version or service is released and ends when it's no longer supported. Knowing key dates in this release lifecycle helps you make informed decisions about when to upgrade or make other changes to your software.

### Servicing

Customers can choose _Current_ releases or _Maintenance_ releases. Both types of releases receive critical fixes throughout their lifecycle, for security, compatibility, and reliability. You must stay up to date with the latest patches to qualify for support.

The quality of all releases is the same. The only difference is the servicing time frame.

#### Servicing for Current releases

Current releases are the latest stable release and they receive fixes more frequently. During the servicing period, Windows App SDK current release is updated to improve functional capabilities and mitigate security vulnerabilities.

Functional improvements are typically very targeted, and may address the following:

*   Resolve reported crashes.
*   Resolve severe performance issues.
*   Resolve functional bugs in key scenarios.
*   Add support for a new operating system versions.

#### Servicing for Maintenance releases

Maintenance releases are no longer the latest stable release, but they will still receive critical fixes. The bar for fixes in Maintenance releases will be higher than the bar for fixes to the Current release.

After the maintenance period ends, the release is out of support.

## Microsoft support for the Windows App SDK

The Windows App SDK is governed by the [Microsoft Modern Lifecycle](/en-us/lifecycle/policies/modern).

Although the Windows App SDK is currently backward compatible to Windows 10 version 1809, the Windows App SDK is supported by Microsoft only on the [Windows releases still in support](/en-us/lifecycle/products/windows-10-enterprise-and-education).

Support has two key benefits:

*   Patches are provided for free, as required for functional or security issues.
*   You can [contact Microsoft support](https://support.serviceshub.microsoft.com/supportforbusiness/onboarding?origin=%2Fsupportforbusiness%2Fcreate%3FsapId%3D2510d164-8500-6eba-dda3-5b6ade9cad01) to request help, potentially at a cost.

Support is conditional on using the latest Windows App SDK patch update and a supported operating system.

### End of support

End of support refers to the date when Microsoft no longer provides fixes, updates, or online technical assistance. End of support may also be referred to as 'end of life' or abbreviated 'EOL'. This is the time to make sure you have the latest available update installed.

Updates are cumulative, with each update built upon all of the updates that preceded it. A device needs to install the latest update to remain supported. Updates may include new features, fixes (security and/or non-security), or a combination of both. Not all features in an update will work on all devices. Update availability may vary, for example by country, region, network connectivity, or hardware capabilities (including, for example, free disk space).

Your use of out-of-support Windows App SDK versions may put your applications at risk. You are strongly recommended to not use out-of-support software.

## How to get help

*   The Windows App SDK uses GitHub Issues to track bugs and feature requests. Search the existing issues before filing new issues to avoid duplicates. For new issues, [file your bug or feature request](https://github.com/microsoft/WindowsAppSDK/issues) as a new issue.
*   For help and questions about using the Windows App SDK, search for existing questions or post a new question on our [GitHub Discussions page](https://github.com/microsoft/WindowsAppSDK/discussions).
*   Technical support for the use of the Windows App SDK may be available from Microsoft Customer Support Services (CSS). If you're a Premier or Unified Support customer, reach out to your account manager for further assistance. Otherwise, visit the [Support For Business](https://support.serviceshub.microsoft.com/supportforbusiness/onboarding?origin=%2Fsupportforbusiness%2Fcreate%3FsapId%3D2510d164-8500-6eba-dda3-5b6ade9cad01) site to open a new support case for the Windows App SDK.

*   [Install tools for preview and experimental channels of the Windows App SDK](preview-experimental-install)
*   [Install tools for the Windows App SDK](set-up-your-development-environment)
*   [Create your first WinUI project](../winui/winui3/create-your-first-winui3-app)

    register a debug identity via the winapp CLI and launch the app with
    package identity (AUMID).
    Set <EnableWinAppRunSupport>false</EnableWinAppRunSupport> in a
    <PropertyGroup> above to opt out.
  -->
  <!--#if (UseLatestWindowsAppSDK)
  <ItemGroup>
    <PackageReference Include="Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools" Version="$WindowsSdkBuildToolsVersion$" />
    <PackageReference Include="Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK" Version="$WindowsAppSdkVersion$" />
    <PackageReference Include="Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp" Version="$WindowsSdkBuildToolsWinAppVersion$" />
  </ItemGroup>
  #endif -->

  <!--
    Defining the "HasPackageAndPublishMenuAddedByProject" property here allows the Solution
    Explorer "Package and Publish" context menu entry to be enabled for this project even if
    the Windows App SDK Nuget package has not yet been restored.

  <PropertyGroup Condition="'$(DisableHasPackageAndPublishMenuAddedByProject)'!='true' and '$(EnableMsixTooling)'=='true
